# 2026-07-22 OpenClaw productivity-profile dependency

- Filed OpenClaw feature issue #112682 and opened draft PR #112683, now at
  `b13226b58c9`, for config-defined reusable tool profiles. The bounded V1
  contract is `tools.profiles.<id> = { baseProfile, alsoAllow?, deny? }`, where
  `baseProfile` must be built in; there is no custom inheritance graph. Runtime
  consumers and doctor diagnostics share one resolver, and the Gateway catalog
  uses the same configured registry. Exact amended-head proof passes 326
  focused tests plus the final 83-test resolver/schema rerun, all production
  typechecks, core/UI test typechecks, changed-file type-aware lint, formatting,
  docs/schema baselines, CLI round-trip, and non-built-in-base rejection. Final
  Codex review is clean after correcting one stale type comment. The draft is
  intentionally blocked: `src/security/**` is secops-owned, and the plugin-trust
  audit needs CODEOWNERS approval before it can receive configured definitions;
  without that small integration a restrictive custom profile can emit a false
  permissive-policy warning. Maintainer edits are enabled. The fresh exact-head
  ClawSweeper review now marks the real-behavior proof `sufficient` with no
  contributor action needed, but keeps a P1 merge blocker for integrating
  configured profiles into the plugin-trust audit; its recommended owner is
  `openclaw/openclaw-secops`. Exact-head live Gateway proof
  now shows `agents_list` added through `alsoAllow` returning `ok: true` and
  built-in `session_status` removed through `deny` returning `not_found`, with
  runtime policy logs naming `gateway-proof`; the loopback proof Gateway was
  stopped. Review is requested from `openclaw/openclaw-secops`, the request is
  visible on the live PR, and the PR body carries the stronger proof. The PR is
  draft and mergeable at exact head `b13226b58c9`; the feature should stay
  draft until secops chooses the audit contract. Lobster #5336 should
  ultimately depend on a
  released form of this generic contract; #112473 remains the independent
  built-in alternative until
  maintainers choose the upstream direction.
- Opened draft OpenClaw PR #112473 for the generic built-in `productivity`
  tool profile. Rebased it onto current `origin/main`, resolved the docs config
  path to the current `agents.entries.*.tools.profile` spelling, and
  force-pushed with an exact old-head lease at `5a99a537cf2`. The PR is again
  mergeable and remains draft. Exact-head focused proof passes 5 tool-catalog
  tests and 147 config-CLI tests; `git diff --check origin/main...HEAD` is
  clean. All 14 changed files pass `oxfmt`, all 11 changed TypeScript files

- Lobster must consume this through the normal released, quarantine-cleared

- Updated Lobster #5336 at `82e1cccca2` to use
  `tools.profile="productivity"` plus exact Lobster-owned `tools.alsoAllow`

# 2026-07-17 scalable publisher-feed distribution track

- Opened draft ClawHub wire-contract PR
  [#3147](https://github.com/openclaw/clawhub/pull/3147) at `d659a420`.
  It adds strict official-catalog query/change/reset schemas, exact payload-type
  binding, deterministic normalized filters, complete query/change chain
  validation, typed identity uniqueness, bounded metadata, cursor-cycle
  rejection, and terminal revision coverage. The committed schema package
  artifacts are included. Focused schema proof passes 14 tests; package
  typecheck, lint, format, diff checks, and the accepted/rejected autoreview
  loop are complete. It depends on RFC/spec PR #39.
- Opened stacked draft ClawHub durable journal PR
  [#3149](https://github.com/openclaw/clawhub/pull/3149) at `1df84c40`.
  It journals each current catalog publication as one revision plus normalized
  upsert/remove/metadata rows, exposes indexed 500-row internal reads that
  atomically return either the retained window or `resetRequired`, and prunes
  30-day history in bounded batches with revision markers retired before rows.
  Each revision now persists its own and cumulative change counts, allowing an
  exact range `changeCount` without scanning retained history.
  Focused catalog/cron proof passes 35 tests, root TypeScript and scoped lint /
  format pass, and final autoreview is clean. `ci:static` is blocked by two
  unrelated CLAUDE.md format failures; `ci:unit` passed 4,651 tests and hit 18
  unrelated Windows path/symlink script failures. No Convex runtime was
  targeted because the worktree has no deployment state.
- Opened stacked draft ClawHub signed-change transport PR
  [#3151](https://github.com/openclaw/clawhub/pull/3151) at `ee662222`.
  It serves `/api/v1/feeds/plugins/changes` as signed DSSE change pages, pins
  the first request's exact sequence range, and protects continuations with a
  five-minute signer-bound cursor carrying the range, exact count, Convex
  cursor, limit, page/offset, and expiry. Retention gaps return a signed 409
  reset to the signed atomic plugin snapshot. The route enforces 500-record and
  1 MiB bounds. Focused journal/schema/signer/handler proof passes 39 tests;
  root TypeScript, lint, targeted format, diff check, and structured autoreview
  are clean. Automatic CI is running; the fork Vercel authorization context is
  expected. The draft includes #3147, #3149, and #3005 until dependencies land.
- Next main-catalog slices: indexed signed query projection; then complete
  sharded snapshots and removal of the current 1,000-entry skills cap.
  The clean next worktree is
  `C:\src\openclaw-clawhub-catalog-feed-indexed-query` on
  `feat/catalog-feed-indexed-query`, based at #3151. Its design must materialize
  a revision-pinned indexed query result with an exact `resultCount`; do not
  regress to filtering the full snapshot or expose an approximate count.

- Updated RFC/spec PR [openclaw/rfcs#39](https://github.com/openclaw/rfcs/pull/39)
  at `39b0295` with the reusable scalable distribution contract: signed atomic
  roots/shards, signed query pages, signed changed-since pages, tombstones,

- Opened draft OpenClaw gateway lifecycle PR
  [#109407](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/109407) at
  `3fa9b41edf60`. It starts followed-publisher refresh after gateway readiness,

- Opened draft OpenClaw authenticated RPC PR
  [#109461](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/109461) at

## 2026-07-15 independent OpenClaw feeds audit

- Audited the merged hosted-catalog series #95846, #95868, #95877, #95964,
  #95969, #95981, #96155, #96158, and #96194 plus the merged trust series
  #98299, #98316, #98338, and #98350 against current OpenClaw main. The
  cumulative design is coherent and does not need rollback: guarded fetch,
  persistent last-known-good snapshots, source profiles, refresh/entries CLI,
  telemetry, DSSE verification, configured thresholds, signed snapshot
  re-verification, monotonic storage, and operator diagnostics are all present.
- Found one shipped trust-rotation defect requiring a focused follow-up PR.
  After a newly fetched feed verifies with a rotated key set or threshold,
  `official-external-plugin-catalog.ts` reparses the old snapshot with the new
  verification policy before comparing sequence metadata. A legitimate key or
  threshold rotation can therefore reject the new feed and also fail snapshot
  fallback until the old cache is manually removed. Compare monotonic metadata
  against the previously accepted snapshot without requiring that snapshot to
  satisfy the newly rotated policy.
- ClawHub #3005 is not ready as authored. It includes unrelated dynamic
  all/official/community/reviewed feed lanes and signs a request-time projection
  from `internal.feeds.rootFeed`; this bypasses ClawHub's existing immutable
  `catalogFeedPublications` payload, exact package integrity, validators,
  expiry, and monotonic publication sequence. Codex review also found that its
  derived sequence can decrease after deletion/demotion and its sparse skill
  scan can omit entries after 5,000 rows. Rewrite/restack it as a narrow signer
  over the exact stored `clawhub-official` publication bytes. Focused ClawHub
  feed-handler tests passed (9 passed); current hosted CI remains red.
- OpenClaw #101981 is not ready as authored. It is still stacked on pre-merge
  trust commits, so its live diff repeats the full trust series instead of only
  the ClawHub default-profile delta. Its bundled trusted-key array is empty,
  while its docs claim a bundled ClawHub key, so normal installs still accept
  an unsigned default feed. Rebase onto current main and populate the stable
  ClawHub key ID/public key after ClawHub generates the production Ed25519 key.
- Reviewed the three merged RFC sidecars. The core spec currently disagrees
  with shipped schema/config on trust values (`unofficial` versus `community`),
  candidate shape, strict optional fields, and `catalog.*` versus
  `marketplaces.*`. The trust spec disagrees with the shipped DSSE envelope and
  key config field names and underspecifies exact PAE, feed-id binding, trusted
  key rotation, and the rotation document wire contract. The account-feed spec
  is conceptually sound but inherits core examples. File one corrective RFC PR
  using the generated ClawHub schema and merged OpenClaw verifier as the v1
  normative contract before calling the specs implementer-ready.
- Current-main focused OpenClaw proof: 53 tests passed; two SQLite-backed tests
  were blocked by local Node 24.14.0/SQLite 3.51.2 runtime safety enforcement,
  not by assertion failures. Supported local proof requires Node 24.15.0+ (or
  22.22.3+/25.9.0+). No PRs, branches, or GitHub review state were mutated by
  this audit.

Patrick's review comments are now visible. Main points: clarify named local package sources and source refs; keep ClawHub owning registry search while clients filter results against feeds; avoid depending on mutable slugs where stable ids are needed; clarify priority/recommended/blocked/disabled semantics and feed composition precedence; be careful with skills because GitHub-synced skills may not have versions and may be indexed by ClawHub but installed directly from GitHub; consider whether regional feeds can be separate edge-cached feeds instead of an in-payload `regions` field; use "marketplace" for the user-facing concept where appropriate; clarify signing and trust-envelope terminology; and note ClawHub publisher labels are currently binary official/not official. Based on this, opened OpenClaw draft PR `https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/95846` from WSL worktree `/root/src/openclaw-feeds-hosted-catalog-prep`, branch `feeds-hosted-catalog-prep`, commit `44c0884c3ed`. The PR intentionally only implements RFC rollout step 1: reshapes `scripts/lib/official-external-plugin-catalog.json` as a feed-shaped bundled fallback, adds parser/type support in `src/plugins/official-external-plugin-catalog.ts`, preserves legacy local catalog parsing, and fails closed for unsupported versioned feed wrappers. It does not add hosted fetch, auth, source profiles, signed envelopes, regional selection, SQLite snapshots, search behavior, or skills. It adds a guarded async hosted loader for `register.openclaw.ai`, conditional request headers, bounded response reads, checksum metadata, schema validation, and bundled fallback while leaving the existing synchronous catalog path unchanged. Then opened draft PR `https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/95877` from branch `feeds-hosted-catalog-snapshot-cache`, commit `accc57e1e5d`, stacked after #95846/#95868. #95877 adds the last-known-good snapshot store contract for the hosted loader: successful hosted loads can write the exact response body plus metadata and save time; fallback paths validate snapshot body checksum, schema, and any caller-provided `expectedSha256` before using the snapshot; missing or invalid snapshots still fall back to the bundled catalog. Local proof for #95877 passed `pnpm exec oxfmt --check  src/plugins/official-external-plugin-catalog.ts  src/plugins/official-external-plugin-catalog.test.ts`, `pnpm exec oxlint  --deny-warnings src/plugins/official-external-plugin-catalog.ts  src/plugins/official-external-plugin-catalog.test.ts`, `git diff --check`, `CI=1 timeout 180s node scripts/test-projects.mjs  src/plugins/official-external-plugin-catalog.test.ts` with 26 tests, `timeout 300s pnpm tsgo:core:test`, and `codex review -c model=gpt-5.5  --uncommitted`. This binds the #95877 snapshot contract to a SQLite-backed OpenClaw state table `official_external_plugin_catalog_snapshots`. The hosted loader now uses the persistent store by default, with `snapshotStore: null` preserved for callers and tests that need persistence disabled. It persists feed URL, body, HTTP status, ETag, Last-Modified, checksum, saved time, and update time, so HTTP 304 or transient hosted failures can use a last-known-good state snapshot before falling back to the bundled catalog. Deliberately not included: source profiles, authenticated feed URLs, signed envelopes, background refresh, search, install UX, skills, or telemetry. Vince is strongly aligned on feeds, primarily for plugins, because feeds give OpenClaw a uniform model for plugin control across companies: enable, block, approve, curate, update, and govern plugin availability without each client inventing a separate distribution channel. The RFC should frame OpenClaw feeds as a shared distribution and governance substrate for plugins first, while still general enough for skills and related packages. The model keeps tenant-approved feeds as the enterprise marketplace lane and uses hosted personal artifacts for user-owned portable skills. Initial scope is intentionally single-file `SKILL.md` artifacts, because the current installer downloads hosted artifacts as one file and does not extract archives. This puts the validated Entra `wids` tenant-admin precheck for the Scout feeds admin UX/routes on `origin/main`; 301 proof can now verify whether Gio's live token carries one of the accepted directory-role ids once the deployment catches up.- 2026-06-29 user-shared named feeds plan: Added `projects/feeds/user-shared-named-feeds-plan.md` as the covering ecosystem plan for viral feed sharing. The model keeps personal feeds as private working/install state, makes the user-facing personal feed an effective view over direct personal entries plus followed named feeds, introduces user-created named feeds with private, people/group-shared, and tenant-visible visibility, and preserves tenant-approved feeds as a separate admin promotion/trust state. The plan covers skills, plugins, policy baselines, MCP servers, connectors, agents, workflow packs, prompt/tool bundles, and future capability manifests. Updated the feeds README and MOS3 feed position note so this becomes the next PR direction rather than treating personal/team sharing as a vague later phase. Follow/include semantics were tightened so following a named feed is stored as a source reference in the personal effective feed, not as a destructive copy into the stored personal feed. The UX plan is now search-first: normal users use `/skills` to search their personal effective feed, follow source feeds from search results, and configure which followed, shared, organization, and marketplace feeds participate in search; tenant admins still use `/feeds` for approval and composition. Reviewed the plan against `.craft/roles/claws` and saved `projects/feeds/user-shared-named-feeds-role-review.md`; role feedback added portability, progressive disclosure, actionable source labels, support diagnostics, audit events, emergency block controls, and a dedicated audit/block/diagnostics PR slice. Final UX clarification: `/skills` search should return mixed result types, so users see matching individual entries and matching named feeds that are shared directly with them, shared with their groups, tenant-visible, or tenant-approved. Added MOS3 storage/search strategy: MOS3 is the desired system of record because it already has adjacent titles, acquisitions, shared/owned titles, ACL, owner, marketplace browse, and query primitives, but we still need to validate whether it has a first-class named-feed/collection object with follow, immutable revision, tenant-visible, mixed search, and audit semantics. Lobster should only bridge missing primitives with MOS3-compatible ids, ACLs, revisions, audit fields, and portable feed documents. Aether evidence is stronger than a generic hunch: its plugin sharing path reads MOS3 title ACLs with `GET /builder/v1/users/titles/{titleId}/allowed?idType=TitleId`, writes them with `POST /builder/v1/users/titles/{titleId}/allowed`, and derives `personal` from `ForAllUsers=false` plus no entities, `shared` from `ForAllUsers=false` plus user/group entities, and `tenant` from `ForAllUsers=true`. That supports using MOS3 title ACL semantics as the candidate visibility model for named feeds, while separately validating whether the named-feed/collection object itself can be represented as a MOS3 title with revisions, follow/include state, mixed search, and audit. Lobster storage review confirms today's Scout feeds are hybrid: live personal feeds can be projected from MOS3 acquisitions, marketplace feeds can come from MOS3 marketplace query, while tenant-approved feed documents, personal overlays, hosted personal artifacts, and tenant admin catalog state are file-backed under configured durable directories. The agent OneDrive path in Lobster is currently the OpenClaw workspace git repo on the user's Graph/SPO drive, not a general feed-document store. A OneDrive-backed named-feed bridge is plausible only if implemented as an explicit feed document/revision/ACL store with an indexed Lobster projection for `/skills`; it should not hide feed state inside the workspace repo.- 2026-06-27 Lobster Scout feeds Skills-page UX PR: Created worktree `C:\src\lobster-feeds-skills-ux`, branch `user/giodl/scout-feeds-skills-ux`, and opened non-draft PR `https://microsoft.ghe.com/bic/lobster/pull/3718` at head `6da2dbe0615b1f81e333a242221b6bc01514c142`. This binds the #95877 snapshot contract to a SQLite-backed OpenClaw state table `official_external_plugin_catalog_snapshots`. The hosted loader now uses the persistent store by default, with `snapshotStore: null` preserved for callers and tests that need persistence disabled. It persists feed URL, body, HTTP status, ETag, Last-Modified, checksum, saved time, and update time, so HTTP 304 or transient hosted failures can use a last-known-good state snapshot before falling back to the bundled catalog. Deliberately not included: source profiles, authenticated feed URLs, signed envelopes, background refresh, search, install UX, skills, or telemetry. The requeued PullRequest run `170807763` passed the runtime E2E shards and Rust MITM canary; Build Validation `170807762` also completed green.- 2026-07-03 followable feeds direction: Decided the next product direction should not share a user's mutable personal feed directly. The safer viral abstraction is followable feeds: user-created named feeds, source feeds such as ClawHub or partner catalogs, and tenant-approved feeds can all be followed into the user's personal effective feed with explicit provenance and trust labels. Personal feeds remain private working/install state. Following ClawHub or another source feed does not imply tenant approval; entries must remain labeled by source and subject to tenant policy. Updated `projects/feeds/user-shared-named-feeds-plan.md` and the feeds README so PR 1 starts with followable feed identity/follow records and MOS3 contract validation, before sharing UI.- 2026-07-03 followable feed storage decision: Named feeds should be MOS3-owned, not a permanent Lobster-only store. Target storage is a MOS3 title or collection-like object for feed metadata, immutable MOS3 package/artifact/feed document revisions for published feed versions, and MOS3 per-user include/acquisition-like follow records if available. ACL should reuse the Aether-observed MOS3 title ACL shape: `ForAllUsers=false` plus no entities for private, `ForAllUsers=false` plus user/group `Entities` for shared feeds, and `ForAllUsers=true` for tenant-visible. Tenant-visible remains distinct from tenant-approved; tenant approval points at a selected revision or entries. If MOS3 lacks a primitive, Lobster can bridge temporarily with MOS3-compatible ids, ACL fields, revisions, and audit fields.
